An omitted destination with no configured default returns `400 { code: "DESTINATION_REQUIRED" }`. An unconfigured or malformed allowlist returns `503 { code: "INGEST_DESTINATIONS_NOT_CONFIGURED" }`. An allowlisted entry whose notebook does not exist on disk (an operator typo) returns `404 { code: "NOTEBOOK_NOT_FOUND" }`, never a fallback to another notebook. All of these reject before any vault write.

**Local share target:** requests authorized by the same-origin exemption rather than a bearer are not external senders and keep the pre-SN-234 capture path — resolve `sectionPath`, else `notebookPath`, else the global Inbox from `ensureInboxSection()`. The installed Android share target therefore keeps working on a deployment with no allowlist configured. Allowlist enforcement is scoped to the credential that public ingress actually uses.

**Response:** `{ page: ApiPageDocument, reference: ReferenceSidecar }` (201). Unauthorized external requests return `401 { code: "UNAUTHORIZED" }`.

### GET /api/ingest/destinations

Token-gated catalog of the ingest destination allowlist (SN-234). It exists so an external sender such as Clear Reader can offer a destination picker without Smart Notes publishing a vault browser.

**Auth:** `Authorization: Bearer <SMART_NOTES_INGEST_TOKEN>` is required. Unlike `POST /api/ingest`, this route has no same-origin share-target exemption — it is an external-sender read surface only. Missing or wrong bearer returns `401 { code: "UNAUTHORIZED" }` with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`.

**Response (200):** `{ destinations: [{ id, label, isDefault }], defaultId: string | null }`, served `Cache-Control: no-store`.

The response deliberately carries no `notebookPath`, no sections, and no notebook that is not allowlisted. Vault paths stay server-side; the caller only ever echoes an `id` back on `POST /api/ingest`. Registered portable notebooks are invisible here unless the operator explicitly allowlisted them.

**Errors:** an unconfigured or malformed allowlist returns `503 { code: "INGEST_DESTINATIONS_NOT_CONFIGURED" }` rather than an empty list, so a misconfigured deployment is never mistaken for "no destinations available".

**Configuration (SN-235 — settings-backed, no env editing required):** the allowlist is operator-configured in-app under Settings → Ingest (`src/components/ingest-destinations-settings-panel.tsx`), not by hand-editing `.env.local`. The owner adds, edits, removes, and reorders destinations and picks the notebook for each from a `<select>` populated by `GET /api/notebooks` — the field is a picker, never a free-typed path, so a saved destination can only ever reference a notebook that actually exists (the exact SN-234 typo-then-404 failure mode this closes off). At most one destination is default; the UI enforces this structurally with a single selected default rather than a per-row flag.

The picked list is persisted server-side as JSON at `<state dir>/ingest-destinations-settings.json` — same pattern as `vault-backup-settings.json` (`src/server/vault/backup.ts`). `loadIngestDestinationsSettings()` / `saveIngestDestinationsSettings()` / `validateIngestDestinationsSettings()` in `src/server/ingest-destinations.ts` own reads, writes, and validation; `loadIngestDestinationCatalog()` (used by this route and by `POST /api/ingest`'s allowlist resolution) now reads through that store instead of `process.env` directly.

**In-app settings API:** `GET /api/ingest/settings` returns the full settings-store rows (including `notebookPath`, unlike the token-gated catalog above — this route is session-trusted, not bearer-gated, and is not meant to be reachable by an external sender, matching `/api/backup`). `PUT /api/ingest/settings` validates and replaces the whole destination list in one call: id required and unique (case-insensitive), label required, `notebookPath` checked server-side against `readVaultTree()` (never trusting a client-supplied notebook list) rather than the request body, invalid input rejected with `400 { code: "INVALID_INGEST_DESTINATIONS" }` and nothing written. A saved change is visible on the very next `GET /api/ingest/destinations` call — no server restart required.

**SN-234 env var precedence (one-time seed, not a live override):** `SMART_NOTES_INGEST_DESTINATIONS` / `SMART_NOTES_INGEST_DEFAULT_DESTINATION` keep working exactly as SN-234 documented them, but only to seed the settings store the first time it is read and no settings file exists yet for that state dir — the seed is written to disk immediately, becoming the persisted store from then on. After that first read the settings file is the sole source of truth: further `.env.local` edits are ignored, even across a server restart. This means a deployment that has never opened Settings → Ingest keeps working unmodified off `.env.local`, while a deployment that has configured destinations in-app can never have that configuration silently reverted by a stale env var. Deleting the settings file (or pointing `SMART_NOTES_STATE_DIR` at a fresh directory) re-arms the env-var seed.

- `notebookPath` must be a single vault notebook directory name (primary or registered portable). Section-depth paths are rejected as configuration errors, both from the env-var parser and from the settings-store validator.
- Ids are compared case-insensitively and must be unique.
- The default is `SMART_NOTES_INGEST_DEFAULT_DESTINATION` when set and it must name an allowlisted id, or (once seeded) whichever destination the settings UI marked default. With exactly one configured destination that entry is its own default. With several destinations and no default, `defaultId` is `null` and a destination-less `POST /api/ingest` is rejected with `DESTINATION_REQUIRED`.
- Malformed env-var configuration (bad JSON, missing field, multi-segment `notebookPath`, duplicate id, default naming an unlisted id) fails the seed attempt with a `503`, exactly as SN-234 specified, and is never partially seeded into the settings store.

### POST /api/email/inbound

Authenticated, server-only raw-MIME ingress for the Cloudflare Email Worker. The
request must use `Content-Type: message/rfc822` and
`Authorization: Bearer <SMART_NOTES_EMAIL_INBOUND_TOKEN>`. On that authenticated
Worker-to-app request, `X-Smart-Notes-Envelope-From` carries Cloudflare's actual
SMTP envelope sender (`EmailMessage.from`) and `X-Smart-Notes-Envelope-To`
carries the envelope recipient (`EmailMessage.to`). The raw request body is
capped at 5 MiB. Missing token or sender-allowlist configuration returns 503;
invalid bearer returns 401; a missing or invalid envelope sender, a disallowed
envelope sender on non-`jobs@` mail, or a mismatch between the envelope sender
and the single MIME `From` mailbox on non-`jobs@` mail, returns 403. Mail whose
authenticated envelope recipient is `jobs@lucidrss.com` skips the owner sender
allowlist and the envelope↔MIME From equality check (Cloudflare recipient +
Worker bearer remain required) so Gmail CAF auto-forwards and job-board From
addresses can land in the Career Job Inbox;
invalid MIME, missing recipient, or an empty usable body returns 400; oversized
input returns 413. Envelope headers are authoritative only after bearer
validation; arbitrary public requests cannot establish an envelope identity by
supplying the headers alone.

`mailparser` decodes RFC headers and MIME alternatives. HTML is preferred and
sanitized to the editor-safe subset; scripts, styles, event handlers, images,
and unsafe links are discarded. If sanitized HTML has no text, plain text is
HTML-escaped. Attachment parts are counted in email metadata but their payloads
are never written. The first credential-free HTTP(S) URL in the selected,
sanitized body becomes `sourceUrl`; otherwise the reference uses
`mailto:<sender>`.

Subjects matching `[Job]` / `Job:` (capture group becomes the posting title),
or any message whose authenticated envelope recipient is `jobs@lucidrss.com`,
append one Career Job Inbox posting instead of creating a page. All other mail
keeps the page-capture path below.

The first subject comma optionally routes `Notebook name, Note title`. The
prefix must uniquely match a current primary or portable notebook's display name
or vault path, case-insensitively and exactly. A unique match removes only the
first prefix and retains later title commas. Missing, empty, unresolved, fuzzy,
or ambiguous directives preserve the normalized full subject and use the global
Inbox. The `.ref.json` email metadata records the original subject, requested
prefix, matched notebook path or fallback reason, Message-ID, MIME sender,
authenticated envelope sender, recipients, received date, attachment count,
and dedupe kind; references use
`type: "email"` and `tags: ["email"]`.

Idempotency state lives under
`<SMART_NOTES_STATE_DIR>/email-inbound/dedupe/`. RFC Message-ID is primary;
messages without one use SHA-256 of the exact raw MIME bytes. Page, reference,
and dedupe files are staged together under an immutable transaction journal.
The journal directory's deletion is the commit point; its presence after an
interruption causes hash-guarded rollback before the next ingest. First creation
returns 201 with `created: true`; duplicate delivery returns 200 with
`created: false` and the existing page identity. Structured diagnostics use the
`[email-inbound]` runtime prefix and never include tokens or message bodies.

### POST /api/assets

Upload a file to a page's sibling `.assets/` directory.

**Query:** `path=<vault-relative-page-path>`

**Body:** `multipart/form-data` with field `file`.

**Response (201):** `{ asset: { url, path, fileName, isImage } }` — `url` is a `/vault/...` path suitable for `<img src>` or file-attachment chip links.

**Implementation:** `src/app/api/assets/route.ts`, `src/server/vault/assets.ts`.

### GET /api/agent/vault, POST /api/agent/vault

**Plain English:** `/api/agent/vault` is the structured tool endpoint the companion uses for bounded vault operations, including PDF page reads.

**PDF page toolkit (SN-199):** `pdf_read_page` takes `{ href, page }` and returns exactly one 1-based page. `pdf_read_pages` takes `{ href, startPage, endPage }`, permits at most three inclusive pages, and caps their combined returned text at `maxPdfTextChars`; empty pages are identified explicitly. `pdf_page_count` returns metadata without extracting document text. All three accept vault-relative or `/vault/` hrefs, reject missing and out-of-range targets explicitly, and never fall back to whole-document text or `pdfChunkOffset`. Page-scoped reads intentionally do not use the SN-114 25 MB whole-document context limit, so large vault PDFs remain readable one bounded page at a time.

The tool catalog and operating context require companions to use this toolkit for vault PDF page questions rather than shell, direct disk reads, pdftoppm, Computer, or owner paste requests. A paste request is permitted only after the toolkit reports an empty/image-only, missing, unreadable, or out-of-range page.

**Other tools:** Primary page/comment/navigation tools include `page_get`, `page_write`, `page_update_body`, `page_edit`, `page_replace_section`, `page_update_frontmatter`, `page_find`, `page_siblings`, `page_parent`, `comment_list`, `comment_context`, and `comment_address`. Tree mutations use page/notebook/section groups. Jupyter tools remain `jupyter_notebook_context` and `jupyter_cell_create/edit/reorder/delete`.

### GET /api/agent/vault, POST /api/agent/vault

Structured vault operation API for the AI companion (SN-33, SN-84).

**GET /api/agent/vault** — Returns the registered tool catalog (`{ tools, commands }`).

**POST /api/agent/vault** — Dispatches a vault operation. Two call forms:

```json
// Named tool form (preferred)
{ "tool": "page_update_body", "args": { "path": "Notebook/Section/page.html", "body": "<p>...</p>" } }

// Raw command form
{ "group": "page", "action": "write", "args": { ... } }
```

**Path canonicalization (SN-84):** All page paths are canonicalized server-side before use:
- Missing `.html` extension → appended automatically
- Legacy `.md` extension → replaced with `.html`
- Windows backslashes → forward slashes
- Leading slashes → stripped
- Same-machine vault root prefix → stripped

**Registered tools (SN-84):**

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `page_update_body` | Replace Tiptap HTML body only; frontmatter untouched. Returns `contentHash`, `updatedAt`, `resolvedDiskPath`. |
| `page_update_frontmatter` | Merge YAML frontmatter fields without touching the HTML body. |
| `page_edit` | Apply multiple find-and-replace patches atomically in one write. Auto-snapshots. |
| `page_replace_section` | Replace content under a named `<h2>`/`<h3>` heading. Auto-snapshots. |
| `page_write` | Full body replace with optional title override. Auto-snapshots. Returns same confirmation fields as `page_update_body`. |
| `page_siblings` | List sibling pages in the same section. |
| `page_parent` | Return parent page via `parent_id` frontmatter (canonicalizes cross-machine absolute paths via last-3-segment fallback). |
| `page_find` | Search pages by partial title. Returns path + breadcrumb. |
| `vault_tree` | Full vault navigation tree (notebooks → sections → pages). |
| `page_get` | Read page including frontmatter, body, and inline comments. Returns `vaultRelativePath` + `resolvedDiskPath`. |
| `page_search` | Search titles and bodies. Each result includes `recordKey` + `resolvedDiskPath`. |
| `page_render` | Full-page PNG screenshot of a text page including ink overlays, math (KaTeX), and images. Shell alias: `rr`. Renders the **saved on-disk page** (not unsaved in-editor edits — save first if you need the latest). Returns `{ relativePath, vaultUrl, absoluteVaultUrl, absoluteDiskPath, width, height, bytes, warnings }` with PNG stored in the page `.assets/` folder (default `page-render-latest.png`). `absoluteDiskPath` is the provider-agnostic handoff for companion vision: call render, then read the PNG file from disk. `warnings` is an array of `{ code: "missing_asset", detail }` for images that failed to load (missing local asset or dead remote URL) — these render as placeholders and do **not** fail the capture. Args: `path` (required), `scale` (default 2), `fullPage` (default true), `outputName`. Requires Playwright + running Smart Notes server on localhost. |

**page_render / rr (SN-122/SN-128 follow-up):** Companions and shell agents can capture a pixel-accurate page view for vision models. Dispatch via `POST /api/agent/vault` with `{ "tool": "page_render", "args": { "path": "Notebook/Section/page.html" } }` or `node scripts/vault-tool.mjs rr --path "Notebook/Section/page.html"`. Sandboxed companions whose only network primitive is GET-only `WebFetch` must use `GET /api/agent/render?path=<vault-relative-path>` instead; it returns the same render payload and exists specifically because the editor sandbox has no shell and cannot POST to `/api/agent/vault`. The server issues a short-lived, path-bound token, Playwright loads `/render/page?path=...&token=...` (chrome hidden, read-only editor + ink capture mode), captures the full extended page, and writes the PNG into the page `.assets/` folder. Tokens are **not** consumed on first successful validation because Next can validate the same render route more than once during one capture/hydration pass. Returns `{ relativePath, vaultUrl, absoluteVaultUrl, absoluteDiskPath, width, height, bytes, warnings }`. The provider-agnostic vision flow is two steps: render the page, then use the agent's file-reading tool on `absoluteDiskPath`; do not rely on a model-specific URL attachment path. **AV preview inspect:** preview runs `npm run dev` with a dynamic `PORT`; Playwright uses the bound listen port (`global._smartNotesListenPort`) and `domcontentloaded` (not `networkidle`, which never settles under HMR). First render after cold start may take ~60–90s while Next compiles `/render/page`; dev timeouts are extended automatically. Set `SMART_NOTES_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` when the browser-facing origin differs from loopback (capture proxy). Fails with `RENDER_UNAVAILABLE` when Playwright is missing and `RENDER_FAILED` when the server or render route is unreachable.

**Discoverability (SN-122):** `page_render` is registered in `VAULT_TOOL_DEFINITIONS` (so it appears in the `GET /api/agent/vault` catalog and `formatVaultToolsPrompt()`), in the operating-context vault-tools quick list, and in the per-page **Recommended operations** block (`buildAgentContext` in `src/lib/ai-sidebar.ts`) alongside its `rr` alias and return shape. An agent that reads any of those surfaces finds both the tool name and the shell alias together. Note the render targets the **saved on-disk HTML**, so an agent working from unsaved local edits must persist first (via `page_write`/`page_update_body`) before rendering.

**page_render implementation notes (SN-122 fixes):** Three details are load-bearing and were the difference between "always 404 / hangs" and a working capture:
- **Render token store must live on `globalThis`.** Next.js compiles the `/api/agent/vault` route handler and the `/render/page` RSC into *separate* module bundles, so a plain module-level `Map` is instantiated once per bundle and the token issued during render is invisible to the route (every capture 404s). `page-render-token.ts` anchors the token `Map` on a `Symbol.for` global so issue/verify share one store per process.
- **Render tokens must be reusable within their short TTL.** A one-use token can pass the first RSC validation and then fail a second framework-level validation during hydration, causing `/render/page` to flip to 404 or Playwright to time out. Keep tokens path-bound and short-lived, but do not delete them on first success.
- **Capture with `page.screenshot` + measured viewport/clip, never `locator.screenshot({ fullPage })`.** The Tldraw canvas repaints continuously, so a locator screenshot never reaches "element stable" and `page.screenshot({ fullPage })` fights the viewport-resize reflow — both time out. The pipeline measures the document/render-root height, sets a fixed-size viewport, and clips.
- **Capture mode pins the editor viewport height.** In the non-scrolling render route the measured scroll-area height feeds back into `pageFrameHeight` and runs away (16k+ px blank pages). `RichTextEditor` uses a fixed `INK_CAPTURE_VIEWPORT_HEIGHT` and ignores live scroll metrics when `inkCaptureMode` is set.

**page_write / page_update_body response (AC3, SN-84):** Both return `{ page, contentHash, updatedAt, resolvedDiskPath, vaultRelativePath }` so the companion does not need a follow-up `page_get` to confirm a save.

### GET /api/agent/vault, POST /api/agent/vault

Agent-facing vault command surface for the AI companion (SN-33). Dispatches structured vault operations without bypassing comment anchoring, version snapshots, or tree refresh semantics.

**GET** returns the registered tool catalog, full command map, and prompt helper text.

**POST body (tool dispatch):**

```json
{ "tool": "page_write", "args": { "path": "Notebook/Section/note.html", "body": "<h1>Title</h1><p>Content.</p>" } }
```

**POST body (low-level dispatch):**

```json
{ "group": "comment", "action": "address", "args": { "path": "...", "id": "cmt_x", "replacement": "new text" } }
```

**Command groups:** `page` (create/delete/rename/move/list/get/excerpt/write/draft/append/prepend/patch/search/tag_add/tag_remove/frontmatter_get/frontmatter_set/render), `notebook`, `section` (delete requires `force: true` when non-empty), `comment` (list/get/context/resolve/delete/address), `version` (`page_versions`, `page_restore`).

**Math normalization (SN-71):** `page_write`, `page_create`, `page_append`, and `page_prepend` automatically normalize dollar-sign LaTeX to Tiptap math nodes before persisting. Agents may write `$...$` (inline) and `$$...$$` (block) directly in HTML bodies — no raw `data-type` attributes required. Implementation: `src/server/vault/math-normalize.ts`.

**Implementation:** `src/server/vault/agent-commands.ts`, `src/server/vault/agent-tools.ts`, `src/server/vault/page-render.ts`, `src/app/render/page/page.tsx`, `src/components/page-render-view.tsx`, route handler at `src/app/api/agent/vault/route.ts`, shell runner `scripts/vault-tool.mjs` (`rr` alias).

## 5. Data Model & App State

**Plain English:** The app has two different ways of managing note data. The real one reads from and saves to files on disk through the API. There's also an older in-memory mock system that was used in early development and is still wired up in some parts of the code. Understanding which one is active matters if you're debugging why changes aren't persisting.

### Two state systems (and why they coexist)

**1. Vault-backed state (the real system)** is used by `notebook-shell-reliable.tsx` — the main shell component. It:
- Loads the tree from `GET /api/vault` on mount and tree refreshes.
- Applies the SSR `initialVault` immediately when available, then revalidates with live `GET /api/vault?skipCache=1` on mount and on window focus/visibility resume.
- Hydrates page selection directly from the tree payload (`content` + `metadata`) so sidebar clicks do not wait on `GET /api/page`.
- Uses `GET /api/page` for explicit reload and remote-change reconciliation flows that need a canonical reread from disk.
- Saves page content via `PUT /api/page` through the `usePageDraft` hook with 1200ms debounce autosave.
- All CRUD operations go through the REST API, which writes to disk.

**2. In-memory mock state** lives in `src/lib/app-state.tsx` (`AppProvider` + `useApp()`). It initializes from `src/lib/mock-data.ts` and never touches the filesystem. This was the initial prototype shell. It is still imported by some components but is not wired to the main app path in production.

**Rule for agents:** If you are modifying note persistence, CRUD operations, or anything that should survive a page reload — work in the vault-backed system (`notebook-shell-reliable.tsx`, `usePageDraft`, the API routes). Don't touch `app-state.tsx` for production behavior changes.

### The notebook/section/page hierarchy

Smart Notes supports notebook-root pages, section pages, and nested page rows without changing the vault filesystem layout.

Vault tree shape:
- `VaultNotebook.pages` contains true notebook-root page stubs stored directly under the notebook directory.
- `VaultNotebook.sections[].pages` contains section-scoped page stubs stored under real section directories.
- Nested pages use `parent_id` frontmatter and stay in the same physical container as their parent.

Notebook-root and section pages both support custom page order through `_page-order.json`. Within one section or notebook-root container, sidebar page drag can reorder, nest right, or outdent left; those drops resolve through `resolveSidebarPageDrop`, patch `parent_id` when needed, and persist the container order. Creation from a notebook, section, or page targets that notebook root, section, or parent respectively. Text and Jupyter pages share these placement rules.

**Notebook picker organization (SN-232).** The tree sidebar remains the familiar navigation surface: open, non-archived notebooks appear in vault order; notebook expansion remains multi-expand; and picker filter, lens, notebook reorder, groups, and archive actions do not render there. Page Pin/Unpin lives in the tree page context menu so any ordinary page can enter the picker’s Pinned section. The folder button opens the organization picker, which contains all non-archived open and closed notebooks. Selecting a closed notebook preserves the existing reopen behavior.

The picker’s normal grouped list is notebook-only: notebook rows do not disclose sections or pages. Structural filtering still matches notebook, section, and page names, but a section/page match retains only its notebook row. Pinned pages and the Key notes lens are the explicit page-shortcut surfaces; both show page rows grouped or subtitled by notebook without duplicating the normal list.

User groups and the Ungrouped bucket have accessible collapse/expand headers. User-group collapse persists, while the Ungrouped bucket collapse is local to the open picker. A single Collapse all / Expand all action changes every group at once and replaces the obsolete notebook accordion control. Filtering temporarily reveals matching collapsed groups without changing saved state. Notebook drag persists picker order and group membership. Deleting a group never deletes notebooks.

Archived notebooks are absent from the tree and the picker primary list, and remain reachable under the picker’s explicit Archived disclosure. A notebook that is both closed and archived returns to the closed picker state when unarchived. Group, order, pin, and archive actions are UI state only and never call filesystem mutation endpoints.

**Persisted UI-state contract.** `.smart-notes-ui-state.json` travels through `GET/PATCH /api/ui-state` and stores `expandedNotebooks`, `expandedSections`, `expandedPages`, `closedNotebooks`, the compatibility `accordionMode` field, `pinnedNotebooks`, `pinnedPages`, `notebookOrder`, `notebookGroups`, `archivedNotebooks`, and `companionPersist`. `accordionMode` remains server-normalized for older state files but is no longer hydrated or rewritten by the client. Normalization trims and deduplicates paths, rejects malformed entries, keeps group IDs unique, and assigns a notebook to at most one group. PATCH preserves omitted fields. `closedNotebooks` remains mirrored to localStorage as a device cache.

Key notes (SN-229) remain page metadata shown as a gem beside the title. See **Key notes (SN-229)**.

**Moving nested pages (SN-307).** A section or notebook move carries the selected page and every descendant in its current container. Child `parent_id` paths are rewritten to their new locations, the selected page becomes a root when its former parent stays behind, and both containers retain their visible page order in `_page-order.json`. The move copies page files and companions (assets, sidecars, Jupyter folders, app data, and versions), stages originals as a temporary backup, then commits the new order. On a failure, it restores the originals and removes destination copies. Embedded `/vault/` asset links on moved pages are rewritten to their new asset folders. Tree dragging within a container moves a page and its descendants as one ordered block and rejects placements that exceed the nesting depth cap.

**Cross-section sidebar drag (SN-320).** Dragging a page tree onto another section’s header, its empty “No pages yet” zone, or a page row in that section moves the subtree with the same `movePage` path as the Move dialog (SN-307 semantics and rollback). `resolveSidebarCrossContainerDrop` remaps basenames into the destination, refuses same-container drops and basename collisions with a toast, and after a successful move optionally reorders so a drop before/after an existing page lands at that position (empty/header drops append). Same-container nest/outdent/reorder is unchanged. Section reorder via drag, paper-row DnD, and dropping onto notebook-root containers remain out of scope; use the Move dialog when a destination is not a section.

### Key notes (SN-229)

**Plain English:** Any page in the notebook tree can be marked as a key note. A gemstone icon sits beside its title, and the notebook header's **Key notes** lens gathers marked pages across all open notebooks, grouped by notebook.

**Toggle.** The page ⋮ menu (desktop and mobile tree, plus the page right-click menu) offers **Mark as key note** / **Unmark key note**. The action PATCHes `/api/page` with `{ action: "keyNote", path, keyNote }` and reloads the tree.

**Persistence.** Ordinary vault pages store `key_note: true` in YAML frontmatter and omit the key when unmarked. Linked designs keep it in the sibling `.design-link.json` sidecar. Because the marker is vault metadata, it survives reload, tree refresh, relink, and another device on the same vault.

**Lens.** SN-232's lens reads the full open-notebook tree, selects pages whose summary has `keyNote`, and groups them by notebook. A simultaneous structural filter narrows the lens by names. Groups do not affect lens grouping; pinned key-note pages stay in Pinned and are omitted from the lens body to avoid duplicate rows. Toggling the lens never changes persisted expansion state.

**Toggle errors.** Failed mark/unmark calls show a `sonner` error toast.

**Tree chrome.** `VaultPageSummary.keyNote` is derived from frontmatter/sidecar. The tree renders Lucide `Gem` with `text-accent` after the type icon and before the title, including nested and pinned pages.

### Recipe pages and notebook instructions (SN-305)

**Plain English:** A recipe is an ordinary text page whose body says how to produce a recurring artifact (for example *Fit analysis* or *Tailored resume*). Asking the companion to "run Fit analysis here" creates a child page under the current page and fills it by following the recipe. A notebook can also designate one text page as its *instructions* page (for example "never fabricate; resumes max 2 pages"), which the companion follows only while the active page is in that notebook.

**Marking.** The page ⋮ menu (desktop and mobile tree, plus the right-click menu) offers **Mark as recipe** / **Unmark recipe** and **Use as notebook instructions** / **Unset notebook instructions** on text pages only. These PATCH `/api/page` with `action: "recipe" | "notebook_instructions"` and store `recipe: true` / `notebook_instructions: true` in frontmatter (omitted when off). There is no other recipe UI. `setPageRole` (`src/server/vault/recipes.ts`) serializes role changes per notebook and keeps at most one instructions page per notebook by clearing the flag elsewhere.

**Companion tools** (group `recipe`, also allowlisted in the companion bridge):
- `recipe_list { path }`: marked text pages, current notebook first, then vault-wide.
- `recipe_run { path, name | recipePath }`: resolves the name case-insensitively. A current-notebook match wins over other notebooks; multiple matches in the winning scope return `AMBIGUOUS_RECIPE` (pass `recipePath`). Creates a child of the target titled after the recipe and returns the recipe body as `instructions`, the created page and child paths, and a `nextStep` telling the companion to fill the pages without fabricating.
- `recipe_mark` / `notebook_instructions_set { path, enabled }`: the same role toggles as the page menu.

The operating context tells the companion to resolve "run <name> here" requests through these tools. Ask AI → Continue in companion (SN-303) reaches recipes through ordinary companion chat; there is no separate recipe entry point.

**Templates.** A recipe may link a template page with a normal link labelled `Template` (or `rel="template"`). Accepted forms are `/vault/<path>`, `?page=` / `?path=` query links, and paths relative to the recipe. External URLs are rejected. `recipe_template` frontmatter may instead hold a vault path. When a template is linked, `copyPageTree` (`src/server/vault/page-copy.ts`) copies it and its descendants under the target. The copy includes page type, sidecars, and `.assets`, and rewrites links to copied pages and assets. Copies drop the `recipe`, `recipe_template`, `notebook_instructions`, and `comments` metadata. The copy is planned first and rejected with `DEPTH_CAP` before any page is created if the subtree would exceed the two-level nesting cap at the target. A mid-copy failure deletes only the pages that run created. `page-copy.ts` is the single shared subtree-copy helper; SN-99 page duplication should reuse it.

**Notebook instructions scoping.** `buildCompanionPageContext` returns `notebookInstructions` resolved from the active page's notebook on every turn. The client appends it to that turn's operating context with an explicit "discard prior notebook policies" line, so switching notebooks never carries a policy over. Pages in other notebooks get an empty string. If hand-edited frontmatter leaves several instructions pages in one notebook, none are applied and the context carries a warning instead of failing.

### usePageDraft (autosave hook)

`src/hooks/use-page-draft.ts` manages the draft lifecycle for the active page:

- **States:** `idle -> dirty -> saving -> saved -> idle`
- **Debounce:** 1200ms after the last change before save is triggered.
- **Baseline tracking:** Uses a `baselineRef` to detect dirty state without React re-renders on every keystroke. `latestRef` tracks the current draft content.
- **Flush:** `flush()` immediately saves any pending changes. Called by the shell before navigating away.
- **In-flight deduplication:** If a save is already in-flight and new changes come in, the next save is queued after the in-flight one completes.
- **Race safety:** If a rename changes the path mid-edit, the `selectedPathRef` prevents saving to the old path.

Vault write client helpers use `vaultWriteFetch()` from `src/lib/connection-status.ts` for mutating API calls. A network error or non-2xx write response raises the sticky `Connection lost — changes may not be saving` signal shown in the editor top bar. The signal lives in a module-level singleton so it survives page switches and component remounts, and it clears only after a later successful write response. The top bar also shows compact Socket.IO sync state (`Connected / Idle`, `Connecting`, or `Disconnected`) beside the save status.

The reliable shell tags text-page autosaves and version restores with both the current Socket.IO socket id and a tab-local client id. Same-tab `file_updated` echoes are ignored and do not cancel pending autosave timers. For active, non-self `file_updated` events, the shell cancels any pending autosave timer before fetching disk so an old local save cannot overwrite the external update while detection is in flight. Remote update detection compares the fetched disk page against the draft snapshot that was visible when detection started, so typing that happens while the async fetch is in flight does not create a false external-update prompt. Genuine originless or other-tab page updates still create a guarded reload prompt for the active page.

The reliable shell also has an in-place refresh-note path. The top-bar sync icon flushes pending local edits, re-fetches the active page through `GET /api/page`, replaces the tree entry, rehydrates the editor content, and does not reload the Next.js app. Ink notes remount their canvas to re-fetch the sidecar scene.

**Remote page reload safety:** Clicking Reload for a pending page update re-fetches the active page and rehydrates from disk only when the visible draft has no unsaved local changes. If the user typed after the last saved snapshot, the shell opens a confirmation dialog explaining that reload will discard those local edits; it does not silently replace the editor state.

**Vault structure freshness (SN-91):** The shell listens for `vault_updated` and attempts an automatic `GET /api/vault?skipCache=1` tree refresh while preserving the active page selection. Automatic refresh is allowed only when no save is active, no autosave timer is pending, and the active draft matches its last saved snapshot. Otherwise it sets the pending vault reload state so the owner has an explicit reload action instead of risking local edit loss.

### Lessons Learned

- **`content` vs `body` in the API.** The PUT /api/page route accepts both `content` and `body` for the Markdown text (`content` takes precedence). This was introduced for compatibility when the reliable shell switched from `body` to `content`. New code should use `content`; old code using `body` still works.
- **The tree response includes full page body content.** This was a deliberate performance decision ? load everything on first call so page switching is instant. If the vault grows very large (thousands of pages), this becomes a problem. The field is called `content` in `VaultPageSummary` even though the type name suggests it's a summary.
- **Selection metadata now rides with the tree.** `GET /api/vault` also carries page `metadata`, and the reliable shell hydrates drafts from that tree payload instead of doing a per-click `GET /api/page`. If a future feature needs extra page fields during selection, prefer extending the tree payload over reintroducing an eager network fetch on every click.
- **Optimistic tree selection (SN-53).** Sidebar clicks set `activePagePath` immediately before the async leave/save work finishes, so the active row highlights without waiting on flush/snapshot. Rows expose `data-selected="true"` and `aria-current="page"` for tests and assistive tech.
- **Local tree patch on page create (SN-53).** Creating a page appends the returned `ApiPageDocument` to in-memory tree state and selects it directly. Do not follow create with a full `GET /api/vault` refresh ? that caused visible shell flash and unnecessary latency.

### Bibliography paper identity and references (SN-279)

**Plain English:** A paper is a normal Smart Notes text page that lives once in the Bibliography library. Each paper has a home notebook chosen when it is created; its breadcrumb reads `<Notebook> / Bibliography` even though the file stays in central storage. Any page can reference it; the reference shows up as a chip in the page body that expands the same live notes inline, and as a paper icon + count on that page's sidebar row. Editing from any place edits the one canonical paper, and renaming or moving pages never breaks the link because the link is a permanent ID, not a file path.

**Storage and identity.** Each paper is `.bibliography/<UUID>.html` with immutable `paper_id` frontmatter and `note_type: text`. The normal page, asset, comment, annotation, Companion and version APIs operate on this canonical path. Paper rename changes the title while keeping the UUID/path; creating pages/sections in, renaming, or moving ownership into or out of Bibliography is rejected (`BIBLIOGRAPHY_OWNERSHIP`). A referenced paper cannot be deleted until its references are removed (`PAPER_REFERENCED`); Companion's `paper_remove` (SN-282) clears them first, then deletes.

**Home notebook (SN-292).** `createPaper(title, homeNotebookPath, sourceLink?)` requires an existing, non-Bibliography notebook (`INVALID_NOTEBOOK` otherwise) and writes `paper_home_notebook` (plus optional `paper_source`) frontmatter. The file never moves; `renameNotebook` rewrites matching `paper_home_notebook` values. `paperHomeNotebook()` resolves the home for breadcrumbs, falling back to the first referrer's notebook for papers created before SN-292 (no `paper_home_notebook`). The paper page header subtitle is `<home> / Bibliography`.

**APIs.** `POST /api/papers { title, homeNotebookPath, sourceLink? }` creates a canonical paper; `GET /api/papers` lists papers with their current referring notebook/page locations. The vault tree includes the library as a hidden `.bibliography` notebook when populated; the sidebar filters it out of the notebook list.

**Sidebar tree (SN-292 / SN-325).** Under Open workspace sits a pinned top-level **Bibliography** entry (orange `FileBadge` paper glyph, `--paper` token) that toggles an A–Z list of every paper. Per-notebook Bibliography shortcuts and virtual paper child rows are gone (SN-325). Real nested pages keep their disclosure chevrons; papers do not appear as tree children. Pages that reference papers show a paper icon + count (`PaperCountButton`, `--paper` color) on the page row. Activating that control opens the same central Bibliography filtered to papers referenced by that page (`bibliographyEntries` referrers); Clear filter or toggling Bibliography without a page filter shows the full list again. Papers are created from Insert → **Attach paper…** (final dialog fidelity is SN-293) or Companion; the old sidebar search/filter/New paper panel and the per-page reference picker bar are gone.

**Paper page context bar (SN-292, photo #7).** Below the standard header, a bar shows **Back to topic** · "Bibliography · shared paper page". `paperBackTarget()` picks the destination: the referring page remembered when the paper was opened (chip title / Open full paper), else the last ordinary page shown before the paper, else a referrer (home notebook first); pages no longer in the tree and other paper pages never qualify. With no candidate the button is disabled. Opening a paper from a referring page remembers that page's `.editor-scroll-area` scroll offset (`paper-return-scroll.ts`); **Back to topic** restores that exact viewport after the page reloads via `RichTextEditorHandle.getScrollTop` / `setScrollTop`.

**References.** A reference is a dedicated HTML node: `<div data-type="paper-reference" data-paper-id="UUID"></div>` (Tiptap `paperReference`, atom block). The referring HTML is authoritative; reverse filters and page paper counts are derived from tree page content via `bibliographyEntries`, deduplicated per parent, never nestable, and never persist a second body. Moving/renaming a referring page, section or notebook carries the unchanged UUID. Removing the final reference drops that page's paper count (and its Bibliography filter membership) but leaves the canonical notes and the top-level Bibliography entry.

**Inline expansion (SN-294).** The rounded chip has a chevron, an orange PAPER badge (--paper), and a title that opens the canonical paper through normal page navigation. Its chevron expands an in-flow panel with a teal left rule, read-only rich content, Edit/Collapse controls, and Refresh/Open full paper links. Edit enables a separate Tiptap editor for the canonical paper; edits autosave after a 1.2s pause (or on Save), and every save sends the loaded SHA-256 paper revision with the body, and a fresh GET must confirm the write before local state is trusted. Server page saves are serialized per path and stale inline revisions return PAPER_CONFLICT (409). Refresh and reopen fetch the canonical body. Collapse, Open full paper and parent navigation flush dirty inline edits first; any save failure leaves the panel open and halts autosave until the user saves, cancels or refreshes. The referring page stores only the reference node, and a ProseMirror guard prevents deleting an expanded chip. The editing inline editor contains its caret: Ctrl/Cmd+Home/End and arrow/Page keys at the paper's first/last block stay inside it, and a capture-phase focusin guard (`isInlinePaperFocusEscape`) returns focus to it when the referring editor takes focus without a pointer gesture, so keystrokes meant for the paper never edit the referring page. Entering Edit uses `setEditable(true, false)` so it never writes the paper. The PDF reading shell still uses its own paperInline iframe and flush handshake.

**Evidence.** `tests/papers.test.ts` covers identity through renames/moves, home notebook ownership and rename follow, legacy home fallback, referrer membership, and Back to topic resolution. `tests/page-tree.test.ts` asserts real nested pages stay hierarchical with no paper children. `tests/bibliography-nav.test.tsx` covers the page paper-count control and page-scoped Bibliography filter/clear. `evidence/sn-292/` holds desktop screenshots side by side with fork photos #7, #12 and #17. SN-294: `tests/inline-paper.test.ts` (conditional save, shared refresh, untouched referring pages, 409/400 guards) and `tests/inline-paper-focus.test.ts`; `evidence/sn-294/` holds photo #5/#6 side-by-sides and the browser save-safety run.

**Attach paper picker (SN-293, fork photos #2–#4).** Insert → Attach paper… opens a searchable two-column catalogue with Referenced in, PDF availability, References, and Sort filters plus a filtered/total count. Paper cards show PDF availability, the active vault name, source, referrers, and an Attach paper action. The Create a paper section follows the cards in the same scroll flow, keeping it clear of the grid when expanded. Creating validates title and home notebook, calls POST /api/papers with optional source, optionally uploads a PDF into the new paper using the ordinary asset/page APIs, refreshes the tree, and inserts a paper-reference chip at the caret captured before the modal opened. A PDF upload failure leaves the canonical paper and reference in place and shows an error toast. The picker uses existing design tokens, including --paper for its Insert-menu glyph.

**Promote existing vault PDF (SN-302).** From a page PDF chip context menu (**Promote to paper**), the immersive reader toolbar (**Save as paper**), or Attach paper → Create → **Choose from this page**, the shared `createPaperFromVaultPdf` primitive creates a Bibliography paper, copies the PDF onto that paper as a page asset, and inserts a paper chip **at the PDF's location** on the source page (`insertPaperReferenceAtPdf` — immediately before the original chip by default, never appended at the document end). Title defaults from the filename (editable); home notebook defaults to the current notebook; an optional checkbox **replaces** the original PDF chip in place with the paper chip. If that exact vault PDF asset is already on a paper, the flow attaches/references the existing paper instead of duplicating (still at the PDF location). Companion exposes the same path as `paper_create_from_pdf`; `paper_attach_pdf` remains URL-only.

### Lessons Learned

- Each chip node view owns its own React root (`createRoot`) rather than a Tiptap `ReactNodeViewRenderer` portal; portals are bound to the `EditorContent` instance, and shell re-renders could orphan a live chip (stale DOM, lost state).
- Tree reloads that were fetched before a local edit/save are stale for the open draft. `LoadTreeOptions.draftSnapshot` makes `applyVaultPayload` keep the tree update but skip re-hydrating a draft that changed since the fetch began (first-hydration refetch and live refresh both pass it). Without this, a reference inserted right after page load was overwritten and autosaved away.

## 6. Rich Text Editor

**Plain English:** When you open a page, you see a rich editor — not a raw Markdown editor. You can bold text, add headings, create tables, write equations, collapse sections, and check off task items, all by clicking buttons or using keyboard shortcuts. Under the hood, vault text pages persist **HTML**, so the file on disk keeps the real rich layout rather than round-tripping through Markdown.

**Engine:** [Tiptap](https://tiptap.dev/) v3. Vault text pages persist **HTML** via `editor.getHTML()` / `setContent(html)` — no Markdown round-trip on save (SN-8).

**Component:** `src/components/rich-text-editor.tsx`
**Extensions config:** `src/lib/rich-text-editor-config.ts`

### Enabled extensions

| Extension | What it does |
|---|---|
| StarterKit | Bold, italic, lists, blockquote, code, horizontal rule (heading disabled — replaced by HeadingWithAnchorId) |
| HeadingWithAnchorId | H1–H3 with persisted `id` attrs for TOC section anchors (SN-220) |
| PageToc | Single top-of-page table-of-contents atom (`nav[data-page-toc]`); generate/refresh/remove via format bar (SN-220) |
| TaskList + TaskItem | `- [ ]` checkboxes, nested |
| Table + TableRow + TableCell + TableHeader | Full table editing; inserted via the Insert table submenu (SN-299) |
| TableActiveCell | Decoration-only `.activeCell` teal outline on the cell holding the selection (SN-299) |
| TableRibbonSupport | Per-table `gridMode`/`dataMode`/`expanded` attrs, cell `align`, grid-mode cell keys, Data-mode `numericCell` decorations, TSV copy/paste for cell selections (SN-278) |
| Link | Clickable links (open-on-click disabled; links open on command+click) |
| Placeholder | Greyed placeholder text when empty |
| Mathematics | KaTeX math rendering. Inline: `$...$`, block: `$$...$$`. **Alt+=** opens inline compose at the caret with live KaTeX preview (SN-111); **`\frac` + Space** opens a fraction template and **`a/b` + Space** expands to `\frac{a}{b}`. Editing an *existing* equation — inline **or** block — arrows into it (NodeSelection) or clicks it to open the same caret-anchored compose editor at that equation (`data-math-kind` = `inline`\|`block`); the top-of-page dialog is never used for edits (SN-158). Commit (**Enter**) calls `updateInlineMath`/`updateBlockMath` at the node position so the node keeps its inline/block type with no duplicate; **Escape** cancels; the caret returns to the side it was approached from (`resumePos`). Block preview renders in KaTeX `displayMode`. **Alt+Shift++** opens the block-equation dialog for *inserting* a new block equation (`insertBlockMath`); the dialog is insert-only. |
| TextStyle + Color | Text color palette (toolbar) |
| FontFamily | Per-selection font family (Hanken Grotesk, JetBrains Mono, Caveat). Stored as `style="font-family: var(--font-*)"`. |
| FontSize | Per-selection font size (14px / 16px / 18px / 22px). Stored as `style="font-size: ..."`. |
| Highlight | Yellow highlight (`<mark>`) |
| Image | Inline images with width (`width` attr) and alignment (`data-align`: left/center/right); selection BubbleMenu exposes align + width presets; **TipTap native resize handles** on the selected image (`ImageLayout` `resize.enabled`, SN-112) update width live; **NodeView rebuilds when `src` changes** so SN-55 page swaps cannot leave the previous page bitmap visible (SN-280); paste/drop/toolbar upload via `POST /api/assets` with page-affinity abort after await |
| FileAttachment | File-attachment chips (PDF/Office/images) linking to `/vault/...` |
| Markdown (tiptap-markdown) | Paste/import helper only — vault saves HTML |
| FormattingShortcuts | Extra keyboard shortcuts (see below) |
| CommentExtension | Highlight comment anchors via ProseMirror decorations (only loaded when `withComments=true`) |

All extensions are imported from `@tiptap/extension-text-style` (FontFamily and FontSize ship in that package at v3.x). Registered in `src/lib/rich-text-editor-config.ts` via `createEditorExtensions()`.

### Lists and nested markers (SN-317)

**Plain English:** Bullet, numbered, and checklist items always show their markers (dots, numbers, or checkboxes). Nesting with Tab / Shift+Tab changes the marker style the way Word and OneNote do — deeper bullets alternate disc, circle, square; deeper numbers alternate 1/2/3, a/b/c, i/ii/iii.

**Root cause (SN-317):** Tailwind v4 preflight sets `ol, ul { list-style: none }` globally. `.editor-content ul/ol` in `src/app/globals.css` restored `padding-left` but never re-declared `list-style`, so indented lists had no visible markers. Task lists were already `list-style: none` on purpose (checkbox UI).

**Fix:** Under `.editor-content`, non-task `ul` uses `list-style-type: disc` and `ol` uses `decimal`, with nested selectors cycling `disc → circle → square` and `decimal → lower-alpha → lower-roman` (two full cycles cover deep Tab nests). `ul[data-type="taskList"]` keeps `list-style: none`, flex checkbox layout, and strikethrough on `data-checked="true"`. The same rules ship in page-export CSS (`src/lib/page-export.ts`) so HTML export matches the editor. Nesting itself remains StarterKit `ListItem` Tab / Shift+Tab sink/lift — no extension change.

**Evidence:** `tests/editor-list-markers.test.ts`.

### Keyboard shortcuts

Standard browser shortcuts apply (Cmd/Ctrl+B for bold, etc.) plus:

| Shortcut | Action |
|---|---|
| Escape | Blur the rich-text editor (leave caret / dismiss soft keyboard) without navigating away or entering draw mode (SN-221) |
| Cmd+Alt+1/2/3 | Toggle H1 / H2 / H3 |
| Cmd+Shift+X | Strikethrough |
| Cmd+Shift+7 | Ordered list |
| Cmd+Shift+8 | Bullet list |
| Cmd+Shift+9 | Blockquote |
| Alt+= | Inline equation compose at caret (SN-111); arrow or click existing inline **or block** math (SN-158) to reopen the anchored compose editor at that equation |
| Alt+Shift++ | Block equation dialog |
| Ctrl/Cmd+scroll (editor workspace) | Zoom page surface in/out, 50%–200% in **both Edit and Draw modes** (text + ink scale together via CSS transform on the content frame); **middle-mouse drag** pans the workspace at any zoom; **Space+drag** pans when zoomed; **Ctrl/Cmd+0** resets zoom and pan (SN-113) |

`Cmd/Ctrl+K` stays owned by TipTap link insertion while the editor is focused. The reliable shell only opens vault search on `Cmd/Ctrl+K` when focus is outside the editor surface. Modifier+wheel zoom applies only inside `editor-scroll-area` so normal scrolling without Ctrl/Cmd is unchanged. Zoom uses a spacer wrapper so horizontal overflow stays scrollable at non-100% zoom.

### Desktop vs mobile editor UI

- **Desktop (Clarity toolbar, SN-42, SN-139):** A consolidated format ribbon above the editor (`data-testid="format-bar"`). Grouping matches the Clarity mock: **Aa** dropdown (paragraph, H1-H3, quote, code block, font & size submenu), bold/italic/strike/inline code, text-color dropdown, pen, list dropdown, link, and **+** insert overflow. Teal accent pill highlights active controls (`toolbar-btn--active`). At the `xl` three-pane breakpoint, the toolbar and editor frame use desktop compact density: 26 px toolbar buttons, tighter separators, `xl:px-6 xl:pb-8 xl:pt-3` editor/ink frame padding, and smaller heading/body scale from the desktop density tokens.
- **Mobile:** A condensed Clarity bar (`data-testid="format-bar-mobile"`) with the same core actions and mobile-only selection BubbleMenu actions for **Copy** and **Comment**. On sub-`md` widths the editor content frame uses `px-3` (12 px) as its horizontal gutter - enough breathing room without crowding collapse chevrons (SN-95). Both the content frame (`data-testid="editor-content-frame"`) and the ink overlay alignment div inside `data-print-ink-clip` share the single `MOBILE_EDITOR_FRAME_PADDING` constant (`px-3 pb-8 pt-3 md:px-8 md:pb-10 md:pt-4 xl:px-6 xl:pb-8 xl:pt-3`) so they stay in sync. Wrapper-origin taps clamp into the ProseMirror bounds before focusing the nearest caret position.
- **Exit edit focus (SN-221):** Escape blurs the editor (caret leaves; soft keyboard dismisses when present) without navigating away or entering draw mode. Tapping/clicking format-bar chrome or separators on desktop and mobile also blurs edit focus - this is the primary mobile exit; there is no separate Done button. Chrome blur is `handleFormatBarChromePointerDown` in `src/lib/format-bar-focus.ts`. Interactive controls are left alone on that pointerdown path; each control keeps selection via its own `mousedown` `preventDefault` (ToolbarBtn runs Bold/TOC/etc. from `onMouseDown`). Do not `preventDefault` format-bar control `pointerdown` - canceling pointerdown suppresses compatibility mousedown and breaks toolbar actions including Insert table of contents.
- **Insert overflow (SN-75):** Both desktop and mobile **+** insert menus include a **Today** action (`toolbar-insert-today` / `toolbar-insert-today-mobile`) that inserts the current local date via `formatLocalDate()` (for example June 20, 2026). On mobile only, **Undo** (`toolbar-undo-mobile`) and **Redo** (`toolbar-redo-mobile`) appear at the bottom of the dropdown after a separator (SN-93); they are disabled when nothing is available to undo or redo. `DropdownMenuItemWithIcon` supports an optional `disabled` prop for this.
- **Insert Image multi-select (SN-339 rework):** The **Insert image** action (both desktop `toolbar-image` and mobile via `InsertOverflowDropdown`) clicks a hidden `<input type="file" accept="image/*" multiple>` (`ref={fileInputRef}`). The `multiple` attribute lets the OS file picker accept a multi-file selection in one gesture on desktop and modern mobile. All selected files flow through `handleAssetFiles` → `processAssetFiles`. The pipeline: (1) filter for supported types; (2) **parallel pre-read** — all files are read into Blobs concurrently via `Promise.allSettled(files.map(readFileForUpload))` (local I/O, no snapshot risk); (3) **serial upload+insert** — each Blob is POSTed to `POST /api/assets` via `uploadEditorAssetFromBlob` and inserted as an `image` node, one at a time, preserving the SN-283 serial-snapshot guarantee; (4) **rAF settle** — `requestAnimationFrame` fires between each image insert so ProseMirror commits the previous transaction to the DOM before the next image fires, preventing intermittent render failures; (5) **batch progress UX** — toasts show `Uploading image N/total…` / `Inserted image N/total` when N > 1. The camera insert path (`CameraSessionOverlay`, SN-340) submits all captured photos through the same `handleAssetFiles` serial queue.
- **Camera capture (SN-140, SN-340 rework 2):** On phones and tablets the insert menu offers a **Take photo** action; on desktop it stays hidden. Both insert overflows render Take photo (`toolbar-camera` / `toolbar-camera-mobile`) only when `supportsNativeCameraCapture()` (`src/lib/camera-capture.ts`) is true — requires the HTML `capture` attribute plus `(pointer: coarse)`, resolved on mount so SSR/desktop never see it. Tapping Take photo sets `cameraSessionOpen` and mounts `CameraSessionOverlay` (`src/components/camera-session-overlay.tsx`). The overlay opens a `getUserMedia` stream (`facingMode: environment`) into a full-screen live viewfinder. Each shutter tap snaps a frame via canvas, creates a `File`, and appends a thumbnail (`data-testid="camera-session-thumbnail-N"`) to the pending strip — photos are **not** uploaded yet. **Add N** (`data-testid="camera-session-submit"`) is enabled when at least one photo is pending; it stops the stream and calls `onSubmit(files)`. Cancel (`data-testid="camera-session-cancel"`) stops the stream and discards pending shots. In `rich-text-editor.tsx`, `handleCameraSessionSubmit` closes the session, waits one `requestAnimationFrame` so the overlay unmounts and the editor regains DOM focus, then calls `handleAssetFilesRef.current(files)` to batch-upload through the existing serial queue. The native `<input capture>` element and the earlier Take-another/Done bar are removed; `getUserMedia` is the sole capture path. Permission denied shows an error inside the overlay.

- **Selection font controls (SN-350):** The floating text-selection chrome shows compact font-family and size selectors only while text is selected. On desktop it keeps just family, size, Ask Companion, and Highlight; mobile retains the extra selection actions in a scrollable row. They use the same font choices and `setFontFamily()` / `setFontSize()` commands as the permanent format ribbon, applying changes to the current selection; choosing Default clears that mark. Font family and size apply to the highlighted selection only. Clear formatting uses `unsetFontFamily()` / `unsetFontSize()`.
- Font family is stored as CSS variable references so the four app-loaded fonts resolve correctly in-app while still falling back gracefully outside.
- **Selection chrome (SN-349):** Selecting nonempty text on desktop or mobile opens the floating BubbleMenu above the selection. Highlight toggles the existing editor highlight mark. Ask Companion captures the selected text and opens the inline Companion question flow with that exact selection as context. The desktop format ribbon remains available; the selection chrome disappears when text is no longer selected. Draw mode still disables selection; returning to Edit and ending a workspace pan restores it. Exiting Draw switches back to Edit immediately: the ink `flush()` (PUT `/api/annotations`) runs in the background instead of gating the mode change, because awaiting it kept the editor `contenteditable=false` with `user-select: none` for as long as the save took (measured ~15 s under load; unbounded on a stalled connection). Gestures that suppress selection (middle-button workspace pan, tree/AI sidebar resize) clean up on `pointercancel` as well as `pointerup`, so a cancelled pen/touch gesture cannot leave `user-select: none` stuck on the scroll area or `document.body`.

### Page title and inline create (SN-326)

A text page shows one large editable title in the page surface, followed by a thin rule and its creation date on the left and local time on the right. The top app bar shows notebook and section location, not a second copy of the page title. Styling reuses the Clarity foreground, muted foreground, border, ring, font, and existing spacing/type scale documented in Docs/design-system/README.md.

PageTitleBar renders inside the RichTextEditor content frame. The title is page metadata; the editor body starts below the title chrome. buildEffectiveEditorHtml removes a leading legacy H1 only when its text matches the page title, preserving a real body heading with different text. The print path continues to use its print-only page header and hides the screen title bar. New text pages focus and select the title input after creation. On blur or Enter, the shell saves the current draft, renames the vault page, and reloads the selection at its new path.

New sections are created immediately with a unique default New Section name, then their visible tree row becomes a focused inline input. Enter or blur renames the section and refreshes the selection; Escape restores the default. Other new page types expose the same create-then-rename input in the tree. Standard page, child page, and top-bar actions share the direct create path. Creation and rename errors appear in a toast.


### Floating text boxes (SN-296)

**Plain English:** Text pages support OneNote-style free-positioned rich text boxes on a canvas beside the main editor column.

**Main flow frame:** The primary typed column is drawn with the same faint 1px border language as floating boxes. It never moves. The outline stays hidden when the main editor is unfocused, including on initial open, and appears when the main ProseMirror column is focused; it hides again on blur. A selection inside a floating box does not activate it. Print keeps the existing outline. Since SN-318 its width can be resized (see below); the default is still `EDITOR_MAIN_FLOW_WIDTH` = 672px. Ordinary ProseMirror blocks are capped at `--editor-main-flow-width`, and floating boxes stay absolutely positioned on the wider canvas. The outline frames the prose with the frame's own left padding on both sides. Its width is the column width plus twice the prose `offsetLeft` (`mainFlowInset`). Before SN-318 the outline started at the padding edge, so the prose ran 24px past its right border.

**Main flow width-only resize (SN-318):** OneNote-style, mirroring SN-316 but outside the TipTap schema. `MainFlowWidthHandle` (`editor-main-flow-resize`) is a full-height 12px hit strip on the outline's right edge with a sticky 8×16 grip. Each pointer move updates a live `mainFlowWidth` state in `RichTextEditor`, which drives `--editor-main-flow-width`. Prose and images rewrap during the drag, and height stays content-driven (no clipped box; the SN-123 page frame is unchanged). The drag divides by the on-screen scale, so it tracks the pointer under workspace zoom. ←/→ steps 8px, Shift+←/→ steps 48px, and Home resets to 672. `resizeMainFlowWidth` / `normalizeMainFlowWidth` clamp the width to 360–1200px; 1200 matches `--editor-table-expanded-max-width`. Expanded tables use `max(token, main-flow width)`, so they are never narrower than a widened column. The empty-canvas create zone and the Insert → Text box fallback X follow the live width. The handle follows the main outline visibility, and stays available while it has keyboard focus or is actively dragging. It is hidden in read-only mode, draw mode, ink capture, print, and below 768px.

**Main flow width persistence (SN-318):** The width is stored per page in frontmatter as `main_flow_width` (a number), parallel to `page_lines`. It is omitted at the default and never written into the HTML body. A drag saves once, on release. A keyboard burst saves once, after a 400ms pause or on blur. The shell's `handleMainFlowWidthChange` updates the draft right away, flushes any pending autosave, then sends `PATCH /api/page` `{ action: "mainFlowWidth", path, mainFlowWidth }` to `setPageMainFlowWidth`. That call is text pages only, rewrites metadata only, and keeps the body byte-for-byte. Body autosaves keep the key. `mainFlowWidthFromMetadata(draft.metadata)` restores the width when the page opens. HTML export takes `mainFlowWidth` from `getExportSnapshot`. A non-default width puts `data-main-flow-width` and the CSS variable on `<article>`, which caps the exported column at that width. The default export is byte-identical to before. Print uses the live editor DOM, so it follows the CSS variable.

**Create / edit:** Click empty canvas beside or below the main frame, or choose Insert → Text box. Each box has an always-visible outline, a Move and Delete tab, a bottom-right corner resize handle, and (SN-316) a width-only handle centred on the right edge. Drag Move or focus it and use arrow keys to nudge; drag the corner to resize both dimensions.

**Width-only resize and content-driven height (SN-316, SN-344):** OneNote-style. Dragging the right-edge handle (`floating-text-box-resize-width`, `resizeFloatingBoxWidth`) changes only `width`; x, y and the stored height attribute are untouched. Every pointer move commits, so the text rewraps live while you drag. Focusing the handle and pressing ←/→ changes the width by 1px, or 10px with Shift. Height is fully content-driven: the `fitToContent` function in the node view (called by a `ResizeObserver` on `contentDOM`) measures `contentDOM.scrollHeight` on every resize event and persists `scrollHeight + 16` (the 14px padding + 2px border of the outer box, box-sizing: border-box) as the new stored height. The stored height attribute is used as CSS `min-height`, so the border always wraps all text. The fix also sets `dom.style.minHeight` directly in `fitToContent` so the border redraws immediately without waiting for the ProseMirror transaction round-trip. Box content uses `overflow-wrap: anywhere`, so long words wrap instead of overflowing.

**Images grow the box:** Inside a box, an image that the user has sized (it has an inline `width`) is allowed past 100% of the box width. Unsized images keep the Tailwind preflight `img { max-width: 100% }` cap, so a large pasted screenshot does not blow up the box. `fitToContent` in the node view (called by `ResizeObserver` on `contentDOM`) measures both the furthest image right edge (for width growth) and `contentDOM.scrollHeight` (for height growth). If an image sticks out, it commits a wider `width` through `fitFloatingBoxWidthToContent`. That function only ever grows the width, so an image works as a minimum width when the box is narrowed. Expanded tables are deliberately left out of the measurement and keep overflowing as SN-278 intends.

**Lesson (stray boxes):** If a drag starts inside a box (an image resize handle, for example) and is released over the canvas, Chromium fires `click` on the common ancestor, the ProseMirror root. That used to hit the empty-canvas create path. `pressStartedInFloatingBoxRef`, set in `handleEditorPointerDownCapture`, now suppresses click-to-create for those presses. The TipTap image node view does not add the `editor-image` class to its `<img>`, so floating-box image CSS targets `[data-resize-wrapper] > img`.

**Empty auto-delete:** Leaving an empty newly created box (click away / blur / caret leaves) removes it automatically. Boxes that contain typed text, images, tables, or other atoms are kept. Focusing Move/Delete/resize chrome does not count as leaving the box.

**Persistence:** Geometry is stored as x, y, width, and height HTML attributes; the height is updated by `fitToContent` on every content change so it always reflects actual content height. Nested paragraphs, images, and tables remain in the same page HTML and the existing page draft autosave. The box does not reflow ordinary page text. Search, Companion, page export, and print receive the box content as part of that HTML.

**Implementation:** `src/lib/floating-text-box.ts`, `src/lib/floating-text-box-extension.ts`, `src/components/rich-text-editor.tsx`, styles in `src/app/globals.css`. Unit coverage: `tests/floating-text-box.test.ts` (catalog `unit-sn-296-floating-text-box`, which includes the SN-316 width, keyboard and image-growth cases, and the SN-344 content-height cases). Desktop browser evidence: `evidence/sn-316/`. Main flow width: `src/lib/main-flow-width.ts`, `src/components/main-flow-width-handle.tsx`, `setPageMainFlowWidth` in `src/server/vault/pages.ts`; unit coverage `tests/main-flow-width*.test.ts(x)` (catalog `unit-sn-318-main-flow-width`); browser evidence `evidence/sn-318/`.

### Page rule and grid lines (SN-297)

**Plain English:** Like OneNote, a text page can show ruled notebook lines or graph-paper grid lines behind the writing. Pick them from the grid button in the editor toolbar. The lines are only a backdrop. They never move your text, are never copied or exported, and are not part of the page's saved HTML. Each page remembers its own choice, so it looks the same after a reload or on another device. A switch at the bottom of the popover, "Always create pages with these lines", makes new text pages in this browser start with the same lines. That default is stored only in this browser.

**Popover (fork photo #14):** `PageLinesPopover` (`src/components/page-lines-popover.tsx`, Base UI Popover) sits at the start of the desktop and mobile format bars (`toolbar-page-lines` / `toolbar-page-lines-mobile`). It is labelled "Rule and grid lines" and uses the Lucide `Grid3x3` icon. It shows teal active state while the page has lines. Sections, in order:
- **Rule Lines**: None plus narrow, college, wide, and college-with-red-margin
- **Grid Lines**: small to very large
- **Rule Line Color**: light blue (default), blue, green, lavender, grey
- **Footer**: the `role="switch"` row "Always create pages with these lines" / "New text pages in this browser only."

The fork has two toolbar buttons (outline, paper) before the grid button. On main the grid button leads the bar; those buttons land with their own stories.

**Model (`src/lib/page-lines.ts`):** One exclusive `style` (`none | rule-* | grid-*`) plus a `color`. They persist in page frontmatter as `page_lines: { style, color }` through `PATCH /api/page` `action: "pageLines"`. The default is omitted from frontmatter. `savePage` spreads existing metadata, so autosaves keep the key. The shell (`handlePageLinesChange`) updates `draft.metadata` optimistically. It then awaits `flushSaveRef` before the PATCH, so the server's read-modify-write never races an in-flight body PUT, and rolls back with a toast on failure.

**Browser default:** stored in `localStorage["smart-notes:new-page-lines"]` (normalized JSON; the key is absent when the toggle is off). While the toggle is on, later picks in the popover update the default. `createVaultPage` adds `pageLines` to `POST /api/page` for text pages only (`pageLinesForNewPage`). Quick Capture and DOCX import do not apply it.

**Rendering:** `pageLinesBackgroundStyle()` returns only `background-*` properties: CSS `linear-gradient`s keyed on the `--page-line-*` / `--page-rule-*` / `--page-grid-*` tokens (documented in `docs/design-system/README.md`). They are applied inline to `editor-content-frame`, so the lines scroll and zoom with the content and never affect layout. The frame also exposes `data-page-lines` / `data-page-line-color` for tests. The margin line sits at `--page-rule-margin-inset`, inside the frame's left padding.

**Lessons:** a global unlayered `button { font: inherit }` beats Tailwind's layered text utilities on `<button>` elements. Put text-size classes on an inner `<span>`, as the toolbar dropdowns do.

### Tables and Insert table (SN-299)

**Plain English:** Insert → **Insert table ›** opens a small side panel where you pick rows, columns, and whether the first row is a header. **Ctrl+Alt+T** inserts a minimal one-column, two-row table with a shaded header row. At the rightmost cell of any row, **Tab** adds a column immediately to the right and moves into it. **Enter** adds a body row below the current row and moves into its corresponding cell. Grown cells are normal editable TipTap cells and persist in page HTML. Tables work in the main page flow and floating text boxes.

**Submenu:** `InsertOverflowDropdown` in `src/components/rich-text-editor.tsx` renders a `DropdownMenuSub` (`toolbar-table` / `toolbar-table-mobile`, options panel `-options`) with `Rows` [3], `Columns` [3], a checked `Header row` checkbox, an `aria-live` `N cells` count, and the primary `Insert table` button (`-rows`, `-columns`, `-header`, `-count`, `-insert` test ids). Inputs stop keydown propagation (except Escape) so Base UI menu typeahead does not steal digits. `src/lib/table-insert.ts` validates dimensions as whole numbers 1–`MAX_INSERT_TABLE_DIMENSION` (50); an invalid value yields `0 cells` and disables the button. Insert runs `insertTable({ rows, cols, withHeaderRow })` at the current selection.

**Keyboard growth:** `FormattingShortcuts` registers the Ctrl+Alt+T shortcut. `TableRibbonSupport` routes right-edge Tab and Enter through `growTableCommand`, which uses prosemirror-tables column/row insertion and places the caret in the new cell. Tab elsewhere retains normal table navigation. Enter grows a row from the active cell, including when Grid mode is active.

**Grid styling:** `.editor-content` table rules in `globals.css` use `--border` for grid lines, `--secondary` for `th`, and the `--editor-table-cell-min-width` / `--editor-table-cell-min-height` tokens so empty cells stay clickable. The active-cell outline comes from the `TableActiveCell` ProseMirror plugin in `rich-text-editor-config.ts`, which decorates the cell containing the selection head with `.activeCell` (inset 2px `--accent`). It is a decoration only and is never serialized. Wide-table local scrolling (SN-178) is unchanged.

**Assumption:** the same Insert submenu opens on mobile with 44px touch targets. The table ribbon and stats are SN-278.

### Table ribbon and selection stats (SN-278)

**Plain English:** When the caret is in a table cell (main flow or a floating text box), a ribbon appears under the format bar, as in fork photo #16: **Rows · Columns · Cells · Select · Grid mode · Data mode · Format · Layout · Data · Expand table**. Below it a status line shows `Count: N | Numbers: N | Sum: x | Average: x | Min: x | Max: x` for the selected cells. Leaving the table hides it. There are no formulas: a cell is a number only when its text is a plain number.

**Ribbon:** `TableRibbon` in `src/components/table-ribbon.tsx` mounts after the desktop format bar (`table-ribbon`) and the mobile one (`table-ribbon-mobile`, 44px targets, scrolls sideways). It reads editor state with `useEditorState` and renders nothing unless `getTableContext` finds a cell at the selection and the editor is editable. Text tabs are Base UI dropdowns (`-tab-<id>`, menu `-menu-<id>`, items `-cmd-<id>`); the three pills are `aria-pressed` buttons (`-mode-gridMode|dataMode|expanded`). Commands that cannot run are disabled. The ribbon sits in the page flow under the format bar, so showing or hiding it shifts the page. That change waits until the pointer is released, so a mouse or touch drag never moves under the pointer (SN-245). The editor scroll area then scrolls by the ribbon height to keep the content still, but never so far that the active cell goes under the ribbon. Hiding or unmounting the ribbon (for example, entering draw mode) reverses that scroll.

| Tab | Commands |
|---|---|
| Rows | Insert row above/below, Move row up/down, Delete row |
| Columns | Insert column left/right, Move column left/right, Wider/Narrower column (±40px `colwidth`, min 48), Auto width, Delete column |
| Cells | Merge cells, Split cell, Toggle header cell, Clear contents |
| Select | Select cell / row / column / table (a `CellSelection`) |
| Format | Bold, Italic, Strikethrough, Align left/center/right (`data-align` on the cell), Clear formatting |
| Layout | Header row, Header column, Fit columns to width (clears all widths), Delete table |
| Data | Sort A→Z / Z→A by the active column (header rows stay on top; numbers before text, blanks last), Copy as tab-separated, Paste tab-separated |

**Modes (pills):** stored per table as `data-grid-mode`, `data-data-mode`, `data-expanded` on `<table>`, so they persist in page HTML. `ScrollableTableView` mirrors them onto `.tableWrapper` for CSS. **Grid mode:** Up/Down and Enter/Shift+Enter move cell to cell; Left/Right move cells at the text edge; Shift+arrows grow a cell selection. **Data mode:** cells holding a number get a `numericCell` decoration (right-aligned unless the cell has its own alignment) and the table uses tabular figures. **Expand table:** the wrapper's max width rises from the main-flow column to `--editor-table-expanded-max-width`. Inside a floating box the table can extend past the box edge. Local horizontal scrolling (SN-178) still applies.

**Stats and clipboard:** `src/lib/table-ribbon.ts` holds all of the logic. `parseCellNumber` accepts `1,234.5`, `$12`, `-3`, `(7)`, `.5` and `45%` (0.45), and rejects anything else, including `=…`. Count is every selected cell, blanks included (photo #16 shows `Count: 1` for an empty cell). Numbers, Sum, Average, Min and Max use numeric cells only and show `-` when there are none. When the selection is a `CellSelection`, the `TableRibbonSupport` extension makes plain-text copy tab-separated. Pasting tab-separated plain text with no HTML table writes from the selection's top-left cell, adds rows and columns as needed, and selects the pasted block. HTML table paste stays with prosemirror-tables. Reordering, sorting and TSV paste are disabled when the table has merged cells.

**Assumptions (photos are silent):** the tab contents above are the proposal from the brief chat. The fork ribbon is not Syncfusion's spreadsheet ribbon (Syncfusion's tabs are File/Home/Insert/Formulas/Data/View), so this is built on TipTap tables. Row height has no resize control because TipTap rows size to their content; "resize" means column width. Mode pills are per table, not per device.

**Delete selection (SN-354):** With a CellSelection spanning complete columns, Delete removes those columns; with one spanning complete rows, it removes those rows. Selecting the entire table and pressing Delete removes the table cleanly. Other cell selections retain normal content deletion. The command uses prosemirror-tables structural deletes, so remaining cell content and selection chrome stay valid.

**Header sort and filter (SN-355):** Hovering a first-row header cell (`th`) of a table with at least two rows and no merged cells shows a compact Sort/Filter pair above the header's right edge; it hides 120ms after the pointer leaves the header and its tools, so headers carry no permanent chrome. The tools are `TableHeaderTools` (`src/components/table-header-tools.tsx`), portalled to `document.body` so they never enter saved table HTML. Sort (`sortHeaderColumnCommand`) reorders body rows only (header rows stay pinned), toggling ascending/descending using the ribbon's `compareCellText`, and stores `data-sort-column` / `data-sort-direction` on the table. Filter opens a menu of the column's distinct body values (plus `All rows` and `(Blank)`); `filterHeaderColumnCommand` stores an exact-match map in `data-column-filters`, and the `TableRibbonSupport` decoration plugin hides non-matching body rows with `display: none` (decoration only; row content is never removed). Filters on column indexes beyond the current width are ignored. An icon tints `--accent` while its sort or filter is active. Tokens: `--popover`, `--border`, `--radius-sm`, `--shadow-popover`, `--secondary`, `--muted-foreground`, `--ring`, `--table-ribbon-button-size/-icon-size`; 44px buttons on coarse pointers.
